# Verifier coverage of the endpoints changed since the last release (2026-10-24)

Release gate (Andrew, 2026-10-21): every endpoint that is new or changed since the last published release is reached by a verifier lane and by a fault-injection arm the verifier or a check provably catches. Range `6f71a54cb..de1723c2f44a086a04c6f966d49342777124133a`. Generated by `tools/verify_coverage_audit.py` (static: the export walk of `tools/public_runtime_inventory.py`, `tools/lane_select.py`'s Mojo definition graph, and a scoped name closure from each lane's own code); the rows marked by hand are the `ANNOTATIONS` and `ANNOTATIONS_BY_SRC` tables in that file, each checked in the source.

Regenerate:

```sh
python3 tools/verify_coverage_audit.py --base 6f71a54cb --json <audit.json> --markdown docs/plans/VERIFY_COVERAGE_20261024.md
```

Public Python methods whose call path changed: 976 covered 976.

`covered` describes static reach only. It does not mean that a lane was run, recorded or admitted. Fault arms marked `armed` likewise describe source reach, not successful fault detection.

## Reach rules (2026-10-24)

- **Native owners.** A migrated public method is one owner call (`xseq_native_call`, `decomp_native_call`); the owner picks its handler on the operation and family strings and calls compiled entries by name (`entries["croston"]`, `self.fn("x_decomp_dev_gemm")`, `"xn_" + name`). `OwnerDispatch` reads every `if`/`elif` on a dispatch variable in the Python-facing Mojo, and the entries its branch and the definitions it calls name (four calls deep, a type's methods followed by name). A lane reaches those entries when its string constants or class names satisfy every enclosing condition with a specific value (`croston_fit`, `FastICA`); a branch on generic values only (`fit`, a package method name, a plain word) is credited only to lanes that reach that owner entry itself, whose own impl file holds the branch. A branch that names another owner operation relays it.
- **Bridge connects.** `_IPCA_CALL, _ = _native_ipca_bridge.connect_public(IncrementalPCA)`: reading the bound name follows the connect and the call's own string arguments (`"als_native_call"`).
- **Python shell.** A public method is API shell (argument checks routing to a native call; the no-Python-compute fence keeps arithmetic out of it). It is covered when a lane reaches it, or, as `covered (via native)`, when every native entry its walked call path reaches is covered. A method with no native work at all (`return self`) is n/a.
- **Constant probes.** A registration whose whole body returns one compiled constant (a contract version, vendor or capability flag) reads no data and computes nothing: n/a.

## Retained reference evidence

RETAINED means that numeric reference parts have linked record metadata in the captured reference table. It does not establish complete fixtures, all vendor columns, installed-package execution, source applicability or admission of this candidate. The JSON preserves the actual parts, columns and source records, including observed values that differ from the saved reference. RETAINED does not mean those columns agree. Intentionally partial records remain partial. OWED means no numeric reference is retained; UNKNOWN means the evidence is unavailable or inconsistent. Candidate admission: **not assessed**.

## Fault-injection arms added (tools/identity_lanes/coverage_sabotage.tsv)

One source patch per changed kernel file that no arm reached, each a value fault in the changed definitions (a float one ulp off, an index permuted, a byte flipped), applied only to a scratch copy. `python3 tools/coverage_sabotage.py plan --arm <arm>` describes clean, sabotaged and restored runs. `check` requires matching source/device/input metadata, complete fixtures, valid clean results, fault detection in every named lane, and restoration to clean output. Refusals require an exact fault-specific review; missing bindings and other infrastructure failures do not count. Retained execution scope is listed under Arms proven below; a declared arm is not proof it ran.

## Arms proven (tools/identity_lanes/coverage_sabotage_proven.tsv)

The following is retained historical evidence. This static report does not establish its applicability to the candidate's source, binding ownership or physical library layout.

Run one at a time on the Mac (lane run-sabotage-arms, 2026-10-21) over the merged Metal (identical) and host builds of 51bf10f4f, whose kernel sources equal 79982adaa's. Per arm: the patch applied, the named per-binding builds rebuilt (a binding's own file wins over the merged one), the arm's lanes run (`identity_break.py --fixtures base --repeats 1`) on Metal and on the host column (`MOJOLEARN_VENDOR=cpu`) for the columns whose bindings were rebuilt, then the patch reverted and its builds removed and the lanes run again. A lane is caught when a column it runs on MOVED or REFUSED against the clean run of the same lanes; the sabotaged cells are also judged against `python/mojolearn/verify_reference/table.json` (OWED: a new lane with no reference yet, judged against the clean run only; IDENTICAL: a column the fault does not reach while the other column moved). Every REFUSED cell is the fault tripping one of the library's own checks.

Fixed during the run (no lane weakened; every arm whose patch or lanes gained something was rerun and caught; the arms that only stopped naming an unreachable lane keep their run):

- `coverage-core-hotpath_impl_rows`: x-prep-user-objects did not move. The patch swapped fold labels 0 and 1 in both `assign_fold_i64` and `count_fold_hits_i64`, a consistent relabeling no output can show; the swap now stays in `assign_fold_i64` only, so TargetEncoder's `cv=<splitter>` check refuses. The patch also faults the new host `bincount2_i32`.
- `coverage-x_decomp-select_ops`: x-decomp-lstsq-rsvd never reaches the live-column compaction (its sketches are no wider than the rank), so it did not move. New lane `x-decomp-rsvd-rank-deficient` (a rank-4 matrix with an 8-wide sketch; 9 of 9 fixtures STABLE, Metal equal to the host column) catches it, and `coverage-x_decomp-select_dev` names it too.
- Lanes the arm's fault cannot reach, no longer named: par-cholesky and par-hdbscan on the host-binding arms (`coverage-decomposition-linalg_host_impl`, `coverage-hdbscan-hdbscan_door_host_impl`; both read N/A on the host column, which has no multi-device driver), trees-adaboost-reg on `coverage-core-array_helpers_impl` (AdaBoost.R2 predicts by the weighted median and calls none of the faulted helpers).
- A real host-column bug: `StratifiedGroupKFold.split` refused on a CPU-only install because the GPU base binding's `bincount2_i32` and `strat_group_plan_i32` had no host twin. The core host binding has both now; x-metrics-splitters on the host column is IDENTICAL to the reference on 9 of 9 fixtures, and `coverage-core-hotpath_impl` and `coverage-core-hotpath_impl_rows` were rerun with the fix in the clean set.
- `coverage-tokenizer-ranks` also lists `build_tokenizer_fast`: that is the FAST-tier binding, which the verifier refuses, so it was not built; the arm is caught through the host tokenizer.

- 2026-10-22, the seven native-owner arms (coverage-native-*): run on 551dee1ce over the 42a554521 Metal + host build, all CAUGHT (every cell MOVED, restored equal to clean). `coverage_sabotage.py check` now matches import-path module names to the arm's bindings, and the three runs share one option set.


### Arm decisions after the 2026-10-24 run (9b04b92c5: 12 CAUGHT, 9 MISSED)

- `coverage-x_neighbors-host_ops` (fixed arm): connected-components and ocsvm MOVED on the host column; the nearest-centroid lane cannot reach `op_softmax64`, which is registered only in fast builds (NC_PROBA64). The softmax edit is dropped, the expected lanes are the two that moved, and `xn_softmax64` is fault n/a (fast tier).
- `coverage-x_cnn-perm_host`, `coverage-x_cnn-perm_device` (fixed arms): x-cnn-gcn MOVED on both columns (edge-weighted GCN runs both the gather and the transposed weighted view); sage and gnn-options do not take the weighted transpose. Expected lanes: x-cnn-gcn.
- `coverage-resample-gather` (retired, n/a): identical builds compile `resample_gather_gpu` / `resample_gather_narrow` out (RESAMPLE_GPU_GATHER is fast or the opt-in C11 define, the narrow gather fast), so the entries return their not-taken flag and no identical lane can move; the resample fit's arithmetic carries `coverage-core-resample_impl`. The patch and manifest row are removed; the entries are fault n/a.
- `coverage-svm-scale_gamma` (fixed arm, new fault): svc reaches `scale_gamma_limbs` (bindings/svc_kernel_native.mojo `_resolve_gamma`), but flipping limb 0's lowest bit is 2^-149 of S1, below the once-rounded gamma. The fault now feeds a wrong cell to the exact sums (cell 0 replaced by cell 1 on the device, counted twice on the host).
- `coverage-core-dense_coo` (fixed arm): x-cluster-spectral-affinities MOVED on the host column. A GPU install never reaches the base binding's COO scan (the metrics dense entry serves every affinity), so the arm is host only and the GPU `nonzero_f32_fill` row is fault n/a; spectral-precomputed is not an expected lane.
- `coverage-training-mlp_ops_rng` (split; new lane): the RNG half MOVED (samba-untied-dropout-accum) and is `coverage-training-neural_rng`. The MLP doors run only on the unfused gradient route a multi-device optimizer takes (`_mlp_fused_enabled`): new lane `neural-trainer-unfused` runs it with MOJOLEARN_MLP_FUSED=0, and `coverage-training-mlp_ops` expects it.
- `coverage-core-msel_device_host` (new fault, no crash policy): both columns aborted (exit 134; Metal IOGPUMetalSuballocatorFree) because the old fault left the split table and its sums disagreeing, which drove out-of-range indices. A crash is not counted as detection: it is not a verifier report and its cause is memory corruption, not the answer. The fault now yields consistent wrong answers (two gathered rows swapped, one test group moved to train with its sums, every group rotated to the next fold with its sizes).
- `coverage-x_cnn-device_ops` (CAUGHT, accepted): the catch is the lane's own product check (tools/identity_lanes/public_cnn_methods.py:39, predict must equal classes_[argmax(predict_proba)]), the documented contract of CNNClassifier.predict; that refusal counts as detection.

## Method and limits

- A registration is CHANGED when a Mojo definition its export reaches (lane_select's definition graph, by name through imports) differs from the base, moves excluded (the same text at the base under the same name in any file). A shared helper's change therefore marks every export that reaches it.
- A lane REACHES an entry when the entry's name is in the lane's scoped closure: the lane function, its helpers and property probes (the harness's shared helpers excluded), the package definitions those name (resolved through imports, methods of reached classes by attribute name, import-time calls, module tables, `name + "_suffix"` composition), or the public method walk of tools/public_runtime_inventory.py. It is static: a reach is a call path, not a run. New and existing lanes require separately retained execution, reference and installed-package records for each claimed target and option; this table does not admit them.
- A row is ARMED when a definition in its export's closure reads a `MOJOLEARN_*SABOTAGE*` define or is edited by a `*/sabotage/*.patch`. `CPU gate HOST_SABOTAGE` is the arm the CPU identity gate builds and requires to move every covered part; family defines and seam patches are their checks' arms; coverage arms are this audit's (run: Arms proven). Definitions with no value to fault are listed with the reason in tools/identity_lanes/coverage_sabotage_na.tsv.
- `n/a` lanes: no Python caller in the package, the fast tier (the verifier refuses fast), a verifier or build probe, or a dead helper; each with its reason in the row.
